=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a game dungeon comprised of n x n rooms arranged in a grid.
You are given a 2D array fruits of size n x n, where fruits[i][j] represents the number of fruits in the room (i, j). Three children will play in the game dungeon, with initial positions at the corner rooms (0, 0), (0, n - 1), and (n - 1, 0).
The children will make exactly n - 1 moves according to the following rules to reach the room (n - 1, n - 1):

The child starting from (0, 0) must move from their current room (i, j) to one of the rooms (i + 1, j + 1), (i + 1, j), and (i, j + 1) if the target room exists.
The child starting from (0, n - 1) must move from their current room (i, j) to one of the rooms (i + 1, j - 1), (i + 1, j), and (i + 1, j + 1) if the target room exists.
The child starting from (n - 1, 0) must move from their current room (i, j) to one of the rooms (i - 1, j + 1), (i, j + 1), and (i + 1, j + 1) if the target room exists.

When a child enters a room, they will collect all the fruits there. If two or more children enter the same room, only one child will collect the fruits, and the room will be emptied after they leave.
Return the maximum number of fruits the children can collect from the dungeon.
 
Example 1:

Input: fruits = [[1,2,3,4],[5,6,8,7],[9,10,11,12],[13,14,15,16]]
Output: 100
Explanation:

In this example:

The 1^st child (green) moves on the path (0,0) -> (1,1) -> (2,2) -> (3, 3).
The 2^nd child (red) moves on the path (0,3) -> (1,2) -> (2,3) -> (3, 3).
The 3^rd child (blue) moves on the path (3,0) -> (3,1) -> (3,2) -> (3, 3).

In total they collect 1 + 6 + 11 + 16 + 4 + 8 + 12 + 13 + 14 + 15 = 100 fruits.

Example 2:

Input: fruits = [[1,1],[1,1]]
Output: 4
Explanation:
In this example:

The 1^st child moves on the path (0,0) -> (1,1).
The 2^nd child moves on the path (0,1) -> (1,1).
The 3^rd child moves on the path (1,0) -> (1,1).

In total they collect 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 4 fruits.

 
Constraints:

2 <= n == fruits.length == fruits[i].length <= 1000
0 <= fruits[i][j] <= 1000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxCollectedFruits(self, fruits: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, complexity O(n^2) time, O(n) memory. Need implement robustly.

Let's design code.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxCollectedFruits(self, fruits: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(fruits)
        diag = sum(fruits[i][i] for i in range(n))
        NEG = -10**18

        # B: from (0, n-1), row by row
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n-1] = 0  # We'll add fruits at each cell when reached? Need include start.
        # Better initialize with fruits[0][n-1] if offdiag? If diagonal? n>=2 so offdiag. But if n=1 not; constraints n>=2.
        dp[n-1] = fruits[0][n-1] if 0 != n-1 else 0
        # Actually if we set diagonal zero, start (0,n-1) offdiag. For n=2, yes.
        for i in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            for j in range(n):
                best = NEG
                if j > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[j-1])
                best = max(best, dp[j])
                if j+1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[j+1])
                if best == NEG:
                    continue
                val = fruits[i][j]
                if i == j:
                    val = 0
                ndp[j] = best + val
            dp = ndp
        maxB = dp[n-1]
```

But wait: At row0, dp[n-1] = fruits[0][n-1]. For i=1 transition from dp[j-1], dp[j], dp[j+1]. Since dp only at n-1, current j can be n-2 (from j-1? previous j = n-1 to current n-2 via -1), n-1 (same), or n (invalid). So ndp[n-2] and ndp[n-1] reachable. Good.

But is B allowed to move from (0,n-1) to (1,n) if target exists? n column doesn't exist, so no. Good.

Need include fruits at start row0 only at start. What if B path could start at (0,n-1) and also "enter" other cells in row0? No, exactly one move per row, so row0 only start. Good.

Similarly C:
```python
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n-1] = fruits[n-1][0] if n-1 != 0 else 0  # start (n-1,0), offdiag for n>=2
        for j in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            for row in range(n):
                best = NEG
                if row > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[row-1])
                best = max(best, dp[row])
                if row+1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[row+1])
                if best == NEG:
                    continue
                val = fruits[row][j]
                if row == j:
                    val = 0
                ndp[row] = best + val
            dp = ndp
        maxC = dp[n-1]
        return diag + maxB + maxC
```

Potential issue: For B, at each row i, current column j can be any 0..n-1. But due to start and transitions, some unreachable. We handle.

Potential issue: We set diagonal fruits zero. But what about start/target if n=1? constraints n>=2. If n=2, start B (0,1) offdiag, C (1,0) offdiag. Good.

Thus below states won't contribute to target. Good.

If b same, d-1; if b-1, d-2. So d never increases. Thus below state cannot reach diagonal/above. DP transition allows b_{i+1}=b_i+1, which keeps d same, not increase. So indeed cannot. Thus below states won't contribute to target. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "C DP" similarly: e_j = c_j - j. Transition c_{j+1} <= c_j+1, so e_{j+1} <= e_j. If e negative, cannot reach final 0. So above states (row<col) dead. Good.

Still counted once. Good.

If b same, d-1; if b-1, d-2. So d never increases. Thus below state cannot reach diagonal/above. DP transition allows b_{i+1}=b_i+1, which keeps d same, not increase. So indeed cannot. Thus below states won't contribute to target. Good.

To reach a diagonal state at row t col t (d=0) later, need d increase from negative to 0. But transition can increase d? d_{i+1}=b_{i+1}-(i+1). If b_{i+1}=b_i+1, d_{i+1}=d_i. If b same, d-1; if b-1, d-2. So d never increases. Thus below state cannot reach diagonal/above. DP transition allows b_{i+1}=b_i+1, which keeps d same, not increase. So indeed cannot. Thus below states won't contribute to target. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxCollectedFruits(self, fruits: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(fruits)
        NEG = -10**18

        # First child is forced to take the main diagonal.
        diag = sum(fruits[i][i] for i in range(n))

        # Second child: from (0, n-1) to (n-1, n-1), row increases by 1 each move.
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n - 1] = fruits[0][n - 1] if 0 != n - 1 else 0

        for i in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            for j in range(n):
                best = NEG
                if j > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[j - 1])
                best = max(best, dp[j])
                if j + 1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[j + 1])

                if best == NEG:
                    continue

                val = fruits[i][j] if i != j else 0
                ndp[j] = best + val

            dp = ndp

        max_second = dp[n - 1]

        # Third child: from (n-1, 0) to (n-1, n-1), column increases by 1 each move.
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n - 1] = fruits[n - 1][0] if n - 1 != 0 else 0

        for j in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            for i in range(n):
                best = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[i - 1])
                best = max(best, dp[i])
                if i + 1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[i + 1])

                if best == NEG:
                    continue

                val = fruits[i][j] if i != j else 0
                ndp[i] = best + val

            dp = ndp

        max_third = dp[n - 1]

        return diag + max_second + max_third
```
